					<div id="mw-content-text" class="mw-body-content"><div class="mw-content-ltr mw-parser-output" lang="en" dir="ltr" data-mw-parsoid-version="0.24.0.0-alpha21" data-mw-html-version="2.8.0"><section data-mw-section-id="0" id="mwAQ"><div class="shortdescription nomobile noexcerpt noprint searchaux" style="display:none" about="#mwt1" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAg" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Short description&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Short_description&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Managing versions of source code or data&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}">Managing versions of source code or data</div><span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt1"><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_with_short_description"><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Short_description_is_different_from_Wikidata"></span>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt2" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAw" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Redirect&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Redirect&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Source control&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;other uses in medicine and environment&quot;},&quot;3&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Source control (disambiguation)&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1368532237" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" about="#mwt3" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;templatestyles&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;src&quot;:&quot;Module:Hatnote/styles.css&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;&quot;}}">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+.mw-empty-elt+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}@media print{body.ns-0 .mw-parser-output .hatnote{display:none!important}}</style></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt2" id="mwBA">"Source control" redirects here. For other uses in medicine and environment, see <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Source_control_(disambiguation)" title="Source control (disambiguation)" class="mw-disambig">Source control (disambiguation)</a>.</div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt4" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBQ" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Redirect&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Redirect&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Revision control system&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;the software tool&quot;},&quot;3&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Revision Control System&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt5" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;templatestyles&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;src&quot;:&quot;Module:Hatnote/styles.css&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;&quot;}}"></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt4" id="mwBg">"Revision control system" redirects here. For the software tool, see <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revision_Control_System" title="Revision Control System">Revision Control System</a>.</div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt6" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBw" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Distinguish&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Distinguish&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Change control&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt7" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;templatestyles&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;src&quot;:&quot;Module:Hatnote/styles.css&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;&quot;}}"></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt6" id="mwCA">Not to be confused with <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Change_control" title="Change control">Change control</a>.</div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt8" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwCQ" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;More citations needed <p id="mwCg"><b id="mwCw">Version control</b> (also known as <b id="mwDA">revision control</b>, <b id="mwDQ">source control</b>, and <b id="mwDg">source code management</b>) is the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Software_engineering_management" title="Software engineering management" class="mw-redirect" id="mwDw">software engineering</a> practice of controlling, organizing, and tracking different versions in history of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Computer_file" title="Computer file" id="mwEA">computer files</a> – primarily <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Source_code" title="Source code" id="mwEQ">source code</a> <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Text_files" title="Text files" class="mw-redirect" id="mwEg">text files</a>, but generally any type of file.</p>

<p id="mwEw">Version control is a component of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Software_configuration_management" title="Software configuration management" id="mwFA">software configuration management</a>.<sup about="#mwt12" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Mercurial_1-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;Mercurial&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-Mercurial-1&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-Mercurial-1" id="mwFQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwFg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwFw">[</span>1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwGA">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwGQ">A <b id="mwGg">version control system</b> is a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Software_tool" title="Software tool" class="mw-redirect" id="mwGw">software tool</a> that automates version control. Alternatively, version control is embedded as a feature of some systems such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Word_processor" title="Word processor" id="mwHA">word processors</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spreadsheet" title="Spreadsheet" id="mwHQ">spreadsheets</a>, collaborative <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groupware" title="Groupware" class="mw-redirect" id="mwHg">web docs</a>,<sup about="#mwt15" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-2&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-2" id="mwHw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwIA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwIQ">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwIg">]</span></span></a></sup> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Content_management_system" title="Content management system" id="mwIw">content management systems</a>, such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Page_history" title="Help:Page history" id="mwJA">Wikipedia's page history</a>. </p>

<p id="mwJQ">Version control includes options to view old versions and to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reversion_(software_development)" title="Reversion (software development)" class="mw-redirect" id="mwJg">revert</a> a file to a previous version.</p>

<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" id="mwJw" data-mw="{&quot;autoGenerated&quot;:true}"></section><section data-mw-section-id="1" id="mwKA" aria-labelledby="Overview"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Overview">Overview</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Overview"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>

<p id="mwKQ">As teams develop software, it is common to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Software_deployment" title="Software deployment" id="mwKg">deploy</a> multiple versions of the same software, and for different developers to work on one or more different versions simultaneously.  <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Software_bug" title="Software bug" id="mwKw">Bugs</a> or features of the software are often only present in certain versions (because of the fixing of some problems and the introduction of others as the program develops). Therefore, for the purposes of locating and fixing bugs, it is vitally important to be able to retrieve and run different versions of the software to determine in which version(s) the problem occurs. It may also be necessary to develop two versions of the software concurrently: for instance, where one version has bugs fixed, but no new features (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Branching_(revision_control)" title="Branching (revision control)" class="mw-redirect" id="mwLA">branch</a>), while the other version is where new features are worked on (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trunk_(software)" title="Trunk (software)" class="mw-redirect" id="mwLQ">trunk</a>).</p>

<p id="mwLg">At the simplest level, developers could simply retain multiple copies of the different versions of the program, and label them appropriately. This simple approach has been used in many large software projects. While this method can work, it is inefficient as many near-identical copies of the program have to be maintained. This requires a lot of self-discipline on the part of developers and often leads to mistakes. Since the code base is the same, it also requires granting read-write-execute permission to a set of developers, and this adds the pressure of someone managing permissions so that the code base is not compromised, which adds more complexity. Consequently, systems to automate some or all of the revision control process have been developed. This <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abstraction_(computer_science)" title="Abstraction (computer science)" id="mwLw">abstracts</a> most operational steps (hides them from ordinary users).</p>

<p id="mwMA">Moreover, in software development, legal and business practice, and other environments, it has become increasingly common for a single document or snippet of code to be edited by a team, the members of which may be geographically dispersed and may pursue different and even contrary interests.  Sophisticated revision control that tracks and accounts for ownership of changes to documents and code may be extremely helpful or even indispensable in such situations.</p>

<p id="mwMQ">Revision control may also track changes to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Configuration_files" title="Configuration files" class="mw-redirect" id="mwMg">configuration files</a>, such as those typically stored in <code id="mwMw">/etc</code> or <code id="mwNA">/usr/local/etc</code> on <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unix" title="Unix" id="mwNQ">Unix</a> systems. This gives system administrators another way to easily track changes made and a way to roll back to earlier versions should the need arise.</p>

<p id="mwNg">Many version control systems identify the version of a file as a number or letter, called the <i id="mwNw">version number</i>, <i id="mwOA">version</i>, <i id="mwOQ">revision number</i>, <i id="mwOg">revision</i>, or <i id="mwOw">revision level</i>. For example, the first version of a file might be version <i id="mwPA">1</i>. When the file is changed the next version is <i id="mwPQ">2</i>. Each version is associated with a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timestamp" title="Timestamp" id="mwPg">timestamp</a> and the person making the change. Revisions can be compared, restored, and, with some types of files, merged.<sup about="#mwt18" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-3" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-3&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-3" id="mwPw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwQA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwQQ">[</span>3<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwQg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="2" id="mwQw" aria-labelledby="History"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="History">History</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: History"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>

<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt19" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwRA" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;See also&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:See_also&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Comparison of version control software#History and adoption&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt20" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;templatestyles&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;src&quot;:&quot;Module:Hatnote/styles.css&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;&quot;}}"></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt19" id="mwRQ">See also: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comparison_of_version_control_software#History_and_adoption" title="Comparison of version control software" class="mw-redirect">Comparison of version control software §<span typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>History and adoption</a></div>

<p id="mwRg">IBM's OS/360 <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Support_programs_for_OS/360_and_successors#IEBUPDTE" title="Support programs for OS/360 and successors" id="mwRw">IEBUPDTE</a> software update tool dates back to 1962, arguably a precursor to version control system tools. Two source management and version control packages that were heavily used by IBM 360/370 installations were <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Librarian_(version_control_system)" title="The Librarian (version control system)" id="mwSA">The Librarian</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panvalet" title="Panvalet" id="mwSQ">Panvalet</a>.<sup about="#mwt23" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-4" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-4&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-4" id="mwSg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwSw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwTA">[</span>4<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwTQ">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt26" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-5" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-5&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-5" id="mwTg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwTw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwUA">[</span>5<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwUQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwUg">A full system designed for source code control was started in 1972: the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Source_Code_Control_System" title="Source Code Control System" id="mwUw">Source Code Control System</a> (SCCS), again for the OS/360. SCCS's user manual, especially the introduction, which was published on December 4, 1975, implied that it was the first deliberate revision control system.<sup about="#mwt29" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-6" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-6&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-6" id="mwVA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwVQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwVg">[</span>6<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwVw">]</span></span></a></sup> The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revision_Control_System" title="Revision Control System" id="mwWA">Revision Control System</a> (RCS) followed in 1982<sup about="#mwt32" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-7" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-7&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-7" id="mwWQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwWg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwWw">[</span>7<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwXA">]</span></span></a></sup> and, later, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concurrent_Versions_System" title="Concurrent Versions System" id="mwXQ">Concurrent Versions System</a> (CVS) added network and concurrent development features to RCS. After CVS, a dominant successor was <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apache_Subversion" title="Apache Subversion" id="mwXg">Subversion</a>,<sup about="#mwt35" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-8" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-8&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-8" id="mwXw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwYA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwYQ">[</span>8<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwYg">]</span></span></a></sup> followed by the rise of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Distributed_version_control" title="Distributed version control" id="mwYw">distributed version control</a> tools such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git" title="Git" id="mwZA">Git</a>.<sup about="#mwt38" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-9" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-9&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-9" id="mwZQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwZg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwZw">[</span>9<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwaA">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="3" id="mwaQ" aria-labelledby="Structure"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Structure">Structure</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Structure"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwag">Revision control manages changes to a set of data over time. These changes can be structured in various ways.</p>

<p id="mwaw">Often the data is thought of as a collection of many individual items, such as files or documents, and changes to individual files are tracked. This is in line with the notion of keeping files separate but causes problems when identity changes, which happens when files are renamed, split or merged.  Accordingly, some systems such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git" title="Git" id="mwbA">Git</a>, consider changes to the data as a whole instead, which is less intuitive for simple changes but simplifies more complex changes.</p>

<p id="mwbQ">When data that is under revision control is modified, after being retrieved by <i id="mwbg">checking out,</i> this is not in general immediately reflected in the revision control system (in the <i id="mwbw"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Repository_(version_control)" title="Repository (version control)" id="mwcA">repository</a></i>), but must instead be <i id="mwcQ">checked in</i> or <i id="mwcg">committed.</i> A copy outside of revision control is known as a "working copy". As a simple example, when editing a computer file, the data stored in memory by the editing program is the working copy, which is committed by saving the file. Concretely, one may print out a document, edit it by hand, and only later manually input the changes into a computer and save it. For source code control, the working copy is instead a copy of all files in a particular revision, generally stored locally on the developer's computer;<sup about="#mwt39" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-10" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;note&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-10&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-10" data-mw-group="note" id="mwcw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwdA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwdQ">[</span>note 1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwdg">]</span></span></a></sup> in this case saving the file only changes the working copy, and checking into the repository is a separate step.</p>

<p id="mwdw">If multiple people are working on a single data set or document, they are implicitly creating branches of the data (in their working copies), and thus issues of merging arise, as discussed below. For simple collaborative document editing, this can be prevented by using <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File_locking" title="File locking" id="mweA">file locking</a> or simply avoiding working on the same document that someone else is working on.</p>

<p id="mweQ">Revision control systems are often centralized, with a single authoritative data store, the <i id="mweg">repository,</i> and check-outs and check-ins done with reference to this central repository. Alternatively, in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Distributed_revision_control" title="Distributed revision control" class="mw-redirect" id="mwew">distributed revision control</a>, no single repository is authoritative, and data can be checked out and checked into any repository. When checking into a different repository, this is interpreted as a merge or patch.</p>

<section data-mw-section-id="4" id="mwfA" aria-labelledby="Graph_structure"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Graph_structure">Graph structure</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Graph structure"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwfQ"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Revision_controlled_project_visualization-2010-24-02.svg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwfg"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Revision_controlled_project_visualization-2010-24-02.svg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Revision_controlled_project_visualization-2010-24-02.svg/250px-Revision_controlled_project_visualization-2010-24-02.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Revision_controlled_project_visualization-2010-24-02.svg/500px-Revision_controlled_project_visualization-2010-24-02.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" data-file-width="220" data-file-height="540" data-file-type="drawing" height="466" width="190" class="mw-file-element mw-file-upright" style="--mw-file-upright: 0.75" id="mwfw"></a><figcaption id="mwgA">Example history graph of a revision-controlled project, with the trunk in green and the branches in yellow; the graph is not a tree, however, due to presence of merges (the red arrows).</figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwgQ">In terms of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graph_theory" title="Graph theory" id="mwgg">graph theory</a>, revisions are generally thought of as a line of development (the <i id="mwgw">trunk</i>) with branches off of this, forming a directed tree, visualized as one or more parallel lines of development (the "mainlines" of the branches) branching off a trunk. In reality the structure is more complicated, forming a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Directed_acyclic_graph" title="Directed acyclic graph" id="mwhA">directed acyclic graph</a>, but for many purposes "tree with merges" is an adequate approximation.</p>

<p id="mwhQ">Revisions occur in sequence over time, and thus can be arranged in order, either by revision number or timestamp.<sup about="#mwt40" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-11" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;note&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-11&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-11" data-mw-group="note" id="mwhg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwhw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwiA">[</span>note 2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwiQ">]</span></span></a></sup> Revisions are based on past revisions, though it is possible to largely or completely replace an earlier revision, such as "delete all existing text, insert new text". In the simplest case, with no branching or undoing, each revision is based on its immediate predecessor alone, and they form a simple line, with a single latest version, the "HEAD" revision or <i id="mwig">tip</i>. In <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graph_theory" title="Graph theory" id="mwiw">graph theory</a> terms, drawing each revision as a point and each "derived revision" relationship as an arrow (conventionally pointing from older to newer, in the same direction as time), this is a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linear_graph" title="Linear graph" class="mw-redirect" id="mwjA">linear graph</a>. If there is branching, so multiple future revisions are based on a past revision, or undoing, so a revision can depend on a revision older than its immediate predecessor, then the resulting graph is instead a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Directed_tree" title="Directed tree" class="mw-redirect" id="mwjQ">directed tree</a> (each node can have more than one child), and has multiple tips, corresponding to the revisions without children ("latest revision on each branch").<sup about="#mwt41" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-12" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;note&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-12&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-12" data-mw-group="note" id="mwjg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwjw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwkA">[</span>note 3<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwkQ">]</span></span></a></sup> In principle the resulting tree need not have a preferred tip ("main" latest revision) – just various different revisions – but in practice one tip is generally identified as HEAD. When a new revision is based on HEAD, it is either identified as the new HEAD, or considered a new branch.<sup about="#mwt42" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-13" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;note&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-13&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-13" data-mw-group="note" id="mwkg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwkw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwlA">[</span>note 4<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwlQ">]</span></span></a></sup> The list of revisions from the start to HEAD (in graph theory terms, the unique path in the tree, which forms a linear graph as before) is the <i id="mwlg">trunk</i> or <i id="mwlw">mainline.</i><sup about="#mwt43" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-14" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;note&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-14&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-14" data-mw-group="note" id="mwmA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwmQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwmg">[</span>note 5<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwmw">]</span></span></a></sup> Conversely, when a revision can be based on more than one previous revision (when a node can have more than one <i id="mwnA">parent</i>), the resulting process is called a <i id="mwnQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merge_(revision_control)" title="Merge (revision control)" class="mw-redirect" id="mwng">merge</a>,</i> and is one of the most complex aspects of revision control. This most often occurs when changes occur in multiple branches (most often two, but more are possible), which are then merged into a single branch incorporating both changes. If these changes overlap, it may be difficult or impossible to merge, and require manual intervention or rewriting.</p>

<p id="mwnw">In the presence of merges, the resulting graph is no longer a tree, as nodes can have multiple parents, but is instead a rooted <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Directed_acyclic_graph" title="Directed acyclic graph" id="mwoA">directed acyclic graph</a> (DAG). The graph is acyclic since parents are always backwards in time, and rooted because there is an oldest version. Assuming there is a trunk, merges from branches can be considered as "external" to the tree – the changes in the branch are packaged up as a <i id="mwoQ">patch,</i> which is applied to HEAD (of the trunk), creating a new revision without any explicit reference to the branch, and preserving the tree structure. Thus, while the actual relations between versions form a DAG, this can be considered a tree plus merges, and the trunk itself is a line.</p>

<p id="mwog">In distributed revision control, in the presence of multiple repositories these may be based on a single original version (a root of the tree), but there need not be an original root - instead there can be a separate root (oldest revision) for each repository. This can happen, for example, if two people start working on a project separately. Similarly, in the presence of multiple data sets (multiple projects) that exchange data or merge, there is no single root, though for simplicity one may think of one project as primary and the other as secondary, merged into the first with or without its own revision history.</p>

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="5" id="mwow" aria-labelledby="Specialized_strategies"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Specialized_strategies">Specialized strategies</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Specialized strategies"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwpA">Engineering revision control developed from formalized processes based on tracking revisions of early <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blueprint" title="Blueprint" id="mwpQ">blueprints</a> or <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whiteprint" title="Whiteprint" id="mwpg">bluelines</a><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1333433106" This system of control implicitly allowed returning to an earlier state of the design, for cases in which an engineering dead-end was reached in the development of the design. A revision table was used to keep track of the changes made. Additionally, the modified areas of the drawing were highlighted using revision clouds.</p>

<section data-mw-section-id="6" id="mwqQ" aria-labelledby="In_business_and_law"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="In_business_and_law">In business and law</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: In business and law"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwqg">Version control is widespread in business and law. Indeed, "contract redline" and "legal blackline" are some of the earliest forms of revision control,<sup about="#mwt46" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-15" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-15&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-15" id="mwqw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwrA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwrQ">[</span>10<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwrg">]</span></span></a></sup> and are still employed in business and law with varying degrees of sophistication.  The most sophisticated techniques are beginning to be used for the electronic tracking of changes to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CAD_file" title="CAD file" class="mw-redirect" id="mwrw">CAD files</a> (see <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Product_data_management" title="Product data management" id="mwsA">product data management</a>), supplanting the "manual" electronic implementation of traditional revision control. <sup about="#mwt49" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-16" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-16&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-16" id="mwsQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwsg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwsw">[</span>11<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwtA">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="7" id="mwtQ" aria-labelledby="In_game_development"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="In_game_development">In game development</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: In game development"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwtg">Game development often involves large binary files and teams working together across different disciplines. As a result, game studios use version control systems with good support for large binary files, file locking, and fast synchronization. Common tools include Perforce and several newer cloud-based systems.<sup about="#mwt52" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-17" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-17&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-17" id="mwtw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwuA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwuQ">[</span>12<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwug">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt55" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-18" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-18&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-18" id="mwuw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwvA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwvQ">[</span>13<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwvg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="8" id="mwvw" aria-labelledby="Source-management_models"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Source-management_models">Source-management models</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Source-management models"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwwA">Traditional revision control systems use a centralized model where all the revision control functions take place on a shared <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Server_(computing)" title="Server (computing)" id="mwwQ">server</a>. If two developers try to change the same file at the same time, without some method of managing access the developers may end up overwriting each other's work. Centralized revision control systems solve this problem in one of two different "source management models": file locking and version merging.</p>

<section data-mw-section-id="9" id="mwwg" aria-labelledby="Atomic_operations"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Atomic_operations">Atomic operations</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Atomic operations"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt56" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwww" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Main&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Main&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Atomic commit&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt57" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;templatestyles&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;src&quot;:&quot;Module:Hatnote/styles.css&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;&quot;}}"></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt56" id="mwxA">Main article: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atomic_commit" title="Atomic commit">Atomic commit</a></div>

<p id="mwxQ">An operation is <i id="mwxg">atomic</i> if the system is left in a consistent state even if the operation is interrupted. The <i id="mwxw">commit</i> operation is usually the most critical in this sense. Commits tell the revision control system to make a group of changes final, and available to all users. Not all revision control systems have atomic commits; Concurrent Versions System lacks this feature.<sup about="#mwt60" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-19" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-19&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-19" id="mwyA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwyQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwyg">[</span>14<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwyw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="10" id="mwzA" aria-labelledby="File_locking"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="File_locking">File locking</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: File locking"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwzQ">The simplest method of preventing "<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concurrent_access" title="Concurrent access" class="mw-redirect" id="mwzg">concurrent access</a>" problems involves <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File_locking" title="File locking" id="mwzw">locking files</a> so that only one developer at a time has write access to the central "<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Repository_(version_control)" title="Repository (version control)" id="mw0A">repository</a>" copies of those files. Once one developer "checks out" a file, others can read that file, but no one else may change that file until that developer "checks in" the updated version (or cancels the checkout).</p>

<p id="mw0Q">File locking has both merits and drawbacks.  It can provide some protection against difficult <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edit_conflict" title="Edit conflict" id="mw0g">merge conflicts</a> when a user is making radical changes to many sections of a large file (or group of files). If the files are left exclusively locked for too long, other developers may be tempted to bypass the revision control software and change the files locally, forcing a difficult manual merge when the other changes are finally checked in. In a large organization, files can be left "checked out" and locked and forgotten about as developers move between projects - these tools may or may not make it easy to see who has a file checked out.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="11" id="mw0w" aria-labelledby="Version_merging"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Version_merging">Version merging</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Version merging"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt61" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw1A" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Main&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Main&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Merge (version control)&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt62" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;templatestyles&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;src&quot;:&quot;Module:Hatnote/styles.css&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;&quot;}}"></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt61" id="mw1Q">Main article: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merge_(version_control)" title="Merge (version control)">Merge (version control)</a></div>

<p id="mw1g">Most version control systems allow multiple developers to edit the same file at the same time. The first developer to "check in" changes to the central repository always succeeds. The system may provide facilities to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merge_(revision_control)" title="Merge (revision control)" class="mw-redirect" id="mw1w">merge</a> further changes into the central repository, and preserve the changes from the first developer when other developers check in.</p>

<p id="mw2A">Merging two files can be a very delicate operation, and usually possible only if the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Data_structure" title="Data structure" id="mw2Q">data structure</a> is simple, as in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Text_file" title="Text file" id="mw2g">text files</a>. The result of a merge of two <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Image_file" title="Image file" class="mw-redirect" id="mw2w">image files</a> might not result in an image file at all. The second developer checking in files will need to take care with the merge, to make sure that the changes are compatible and that the merge operation does not introduce its own <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Logic" title="Logic" id="mw3A">logic</a> errors within the files. These problems limit the availability of automatic or semi-automatic merge operations mainly to simple text-based documents, unless a specific merge <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plug-in_(computing)" title="Plug-in (computing)" id="mw3Q">plugin</a> is available for the file types.</p>

<p id="mw3g">The concept of a <i id="mw3w">reserved edit</i> can provide an optional means to explicitly lock a file for exclusive write access, even when a merging capability exists.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="12" id="mw4A" aria-labelledby="Baselines,_labels_and_tags"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Baselines,_labels_and_tags"><span id="Baselines.2C_labels_and_tags" typeof="mw:FallbackId"></span>Baselines, labels and tags</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Baselines, labels and tags"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mw4Q">Most revision control tools will use only one of these similar terms (baseline, label, tag) to refer to the action of identifying a snapshot ("label the project") or the record of the snapshot ("try it with baseline <i id="mw4g">X</i>").  Typically only one of the terms <i id="mw4w">baseline</i>, <i id="mw5A">label</i>, or <i id="mw5Q">tag</i> is used in documentation or discussion<sup about="#mwt65" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-20" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-20&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-20" id="mw5g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw5w"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw6A">[</span>15<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw6Q">]</span></span></a></sup><span typeof="mw:DisplaySpace" id="mw6g"> </span>; they can be considered synonyms.</p>

<p id="mw6w">In most projects, some snapshots are more significant than others, such as those used to indicate published releases, branches, or milestones.</p>

<p id="mw7A">When both the term <i id="mw7Q">baseline</i> and either of <i id="mw7g">label</i> or <i id="mw7w">tag</i> are used together in the same context, <i id="mw8A">label</i> and <i id="mw8Q">tag</i> usually refer to the mechanism within the tool of identifying or making the record of the snapshot, and <i id="mw8g">baseline</i> indicates the increased significance of any given label or tag.</p>

<p id="mw8w">Most formal discussion of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Configuration_management" title="Configuration management" id="mw9A">configuration management</a> uses the term <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baseline_(configuration_management)" title="Baseline (configuration management)" id="mw9Q"><i id="mw9g">baseline</i></a>.</p>

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="13" id="mw9w" aria-labelledby="Distributed_revision_control"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Distributed_revision_control">Distributed revision control</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Distributed revision control"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt66" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw-A" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Main&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Main&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Distributed version control&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt67" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;templatestyles&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;src&quot;:&quot;Module:Hatnote/styles.css&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;&quot;}}"></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt66" id="mw-Q">Main article: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Distributed_version_control" title="Distributed version control">Distributed version control</a></div>

<p id="mw-g">Distributed revision control systems (DRCS) take a peer-to-peer approach, as opposed to the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Client–server_model" title="Client–server model" id="mw-w">client–server</a> approach of centralized systems. Rather than a single, central repository on which clients synchronize, each peer's working copy of the codebase is a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Good_faith" title="Good faith" id="mw_A">bona-fide</a> repository.<sup about="#mwt70" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-21" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-21&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-21" id="mw_Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw_g"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw_w">[</span>16<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQA">]</span></span></a></sup>
Distributed revision control conducts synchronization by exchanging <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patch_(computing)" title="Patch (computing)" id="mwAQE">patches</a> (change-sets) from peer to peer. This results in some important differences from a centralized system:</p>
<ul id="mwAQI"><li id="mwAQM">No canonical, reference copy of the codebase exists by default; only working copies.</li>
<li id="mwAQQ">Common operations (such as commits, viewing history, and reverting changes) are fast, because there is no need to communicate with a central server.<sup about="#mwt72" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Mercurial_1-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;Mercurial&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-Mercurial-1" id="mwAQU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAQY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQc">[</span>1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQg">]</span></span></a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap" about="#mwt71" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAQk" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;rp&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Rp&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;7&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><span title="Page / location: 7"><span typeof="mw:Entity">:</span><span typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>7<span typeof="mw:Entity"> </span></span></sup></li></ul>
<p id="mwAQo">Rather, communication is only necessary when pushing or pulling changes to or from other peers.</p>
<ul id="mwAQs"><li id="mwAQw">Each working copy effectively functions as a remote backup of the codebase and of its change-history, providing inherent protection against data loss.<sup about="#mwt74" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Mercurial_1-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;Mercurial&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-Mercurial-1" id="mwAQ0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAQ4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQ8">[</span>1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap" about="#mwt73" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwARE" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;rp&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Rp&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;4&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><span title="Page / location: 4"><span typeof="mw:Entity">:</span><span typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>4<span typeof="mw:Entity"> </span></span></sup></li></ul>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="14" id="mwARI" aria-labelledby="Best_practices"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Best_practices">Best practices</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Best practices"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwARM">Following best practices is necessary to obtain the full benefits of version control.  Best practice may vary by version control tool and the field to which version control is applied.  The generally accepted best practices in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Software_development" title="Software development" id="mwARQ">software development</a> include: making small/incremental changes; making <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commit_(version_control)" title="Commit (version control)" id="mwARU">commits</a> which involve only one task or fix -- a corollary to this is to commit only code which works and does not knowingly break existing functionality; using branching to complete functionality before release; writing clear and descriptive commit messages, making what, why, and how clear in either the commit description or the code; and using a consistent branching strategy.<sup about="#mwt77" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-22" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-22&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-22" id="mwARY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwARc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARg">[</span>17<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARk">]</span></span></a></sup> Other software development best practices such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Code_review" title="Code review" id="mwARo">code review</a> and automated <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regression_testing" title="Regression testing" id="mwARs">regression testing</a> may assist in the following of version control best practices.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="15" id="mwARw" aria-labelledby="Costs_and_benefits"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Costs_and_benefits">Costs and benefits</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Costs and benefits"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAR0">Costs and benefits will vary dependent upon the version control tool chosen and the field in which it is applied.  This section speaks to the field of software development, where version control is widely applied.</p>

<section data-mw-section-id="16" id="mwAR4" aria-labelledby="Costs"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Costs">Costs</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Costs"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAR8">In addition to the costs of licensing the version control software, using version control requires time and effort.  The concepts underlying version control must be understood and the technical particulars required to operate the version control software chosen must be learned.  Version control best practices must be learned and integrated into the organization's existing software development practices.  Management effort may be required to maintain the discipline needed to follow best practices in order to obtain useful benefit.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="17" id="mwASA" aria-labelledby="Benefits"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Benefits">Benefits</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Benefits"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>

<section data-mw-section-id="18" id="mwASE" aria-labelledby="Allows_for_reverting_changes"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="Allows_for_reverting_changes">Allows for reverting changes</h4><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Allows for reverting changes"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwASI">A core benefit is the ability to keep history and revert changes, allowing the developer to easily undo changes.  This gives the developer more opportunity to experiment, eliminating the fear of breaking existing code.<sup about="#mwt80" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-23" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-23&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-23" id="mwASM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwASQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASU">[</span>18<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASY">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="19" id="mwASc" aria-labelledby="Branching_simplifies_deployment,_maintenance_and_development"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="Branching_simplifies_deployment,_maintenance_and_development"><span id="Branching_simplifies_deployment.2C_maintenance_and_development" typeof="mw:FallbackId"></span>Branching simplifies deployment, maintenance and development</h4><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Branching simplifies deployment, maintenance and development"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwASg">Branching assists with <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Software_deployment" title="Software deployment" id="mwASk">deployment</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Release_management" title="Release management" id="mwASo">release management</a>.  Branching and merging, the production, packaging, and labeling of source code <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patch_(computing)" title="Patch (computing)" id="mwASs">patches</a> and the easy application of patches to code bases, simplifies the maintenance and concurrent development of the multiple code bases associated with the various stages of the deployment process; development, testing, staging, production, etc.<sup about="#mwt83" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-24" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-24&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-24" id="mwASw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAS0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAS4">[</span>19<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAS8">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="20" id="mwATA" aria-labelledby="Damage_mitigation,_accountability_and_process_and_design_improvement"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="Damage_mitigation,_accountability_and_process_and_design_improvement"><span id="Damage_mitigation.2C_accountability_and_process_and_design_improvement" typeof="mw:FallbackId"></span>Damage mitigation, accountability and process and design improvement</h4><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Damage mitigation, accountability and process and design improvement"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwATE">There can be damage mitigation, accountability, process and design improvement, and other benefits associated with the record keeping provided by version control, the tracking of who did what, when, why, and how.<sup about="#mwt86" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-25" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-25&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-25" id="mwATI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwATM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwATQ">[</span>20<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwATU">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwATY">When bugs arise, knowing what was done when helps with damage mitigation and recovery by assisting in the identification of what problems exist, how long they have existed, and determining problem scope and solutions.<sup about="#mwt89" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-26" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-26&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-26" id="mwATc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwATg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwATk">[</span>21<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwATo">]</span></span></a></sup> Previous versions can be installed and tested to verify conclusions reached by examination of code and commit messages.<sup about="#mwt92" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-27" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-27&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-27" id="mwATs"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwATw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAT0">[</span>22<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAT4">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="21" id="mwAT8" aria-labelledby="Simplifies_debugging"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="Simplifies_debugging">Simplifies debugging</h4><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Simplifies debugging"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAUA">Version control can greatly simplify debugging.  The application of a test case to multiple versions can quickly identify the change which introduced a bug.<sup about="#mwt95" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-28" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-28&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-28" id="mwAUE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAUI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUM">[</span>23<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUQ">]</span></span></a></sup>  The developer need not be familiar with the entire code base and can focus instead on the code that introduced the problem.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="22" id="mwAUU" aria-labelledby="Improves_collaboration_and_communication"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="Improves_collaboration_and_communication">Improves collaboration and communication</h4><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Improves collaboration and communication"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAUY">Version control enhances collaboration in multiple ways. Since version control can identify conflicting changes, i.e. incompatible changes made to the same lines of code, there is less need for coordination among developers.<sup about="#mwt98" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-29" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-29&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-29" id="mwAUc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAUg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUk">[</span>24<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUo">]</span></span></a></sup></p> 

<p id="mwAUs">The packaging of commits, branches, and all the associated commit messages and version labels, improves communication between developers, both in the moment and over time.<sup about="#mwt101" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-30" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-30&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-30" id="mwAUw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAU0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAU4">[</span>25<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAU8">]</span></span></a></sup>  Better communication, whether instant or deferred, can improve the code review process, the testing process, and other critical aspects of the software development process.</p>

</section></section></section><section data-mw-section-id="23" id="mwAVA" aria-labelledby="Integration"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Integration">Integration</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Integration"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAVE">Some of the more advanced revision-control tools offer many other facilities, allowing deeper integration with other tools and software-engineering processes. </p>

<section data-mw-section-id="24" id="mwAVI" aria-labelledby="Integrated_development_environment"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Integrated_development_environment">Integrated development environment</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Integrated development environment"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAVM"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plug-in_(computing)" title="Plug-in (computing)" id="mwAVQ">Plugins</a> are often available for <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Integrated_development_environment" title="Integrated development environment" id="mwAVU">IDEs</a> such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oracle_JDeveloper" title="Oracle JDeveloper" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAVY">Oracle JDeveloper</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IntelliJ_IDEA" title="IntelliJ IDEA" id="mwAVc">IntelliJ IDEA</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eclipse_(computing)" title="Eclipse (computing)" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAVg">Eclipse</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visual_Studio" title="Visual Studio" id="mwAVk">Visual Studio</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delphi_(programming_language)" title="Delphi (programming language)" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAVo">Delphi</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NetBeans" title="NetBeans" id="mwAVs">NetBeans IDE</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xcode" title="Xcode" id="mwAVw">Xcode</a>, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GNU_Emacs" title="GNU Emacs" id="mwAV0">GNU Emacs</a> (via vc.el). Advanced research prototypes generate appropriate commit messages.<sup about="#mwt104" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-31" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-31&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-31" id="mwAV4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAV8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWA">[</span>26<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWE">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="25" id="mwAWI" aria-labelledby="Common_terminology"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Common_terminology">Common terminology</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Common terminology"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAWM">Terminology can vary from system to system, but some terms in common usage include:<sup about="#mwt107" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-32" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-32&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-32" id="mwAWQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAWU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWY">[</span>27<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWc">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<section data-mw-section-id="26" id="mwAWg" aria-labelledby="Baseline"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Baseline">Baseline</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Baseline"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt108" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAWk" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Main&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Main&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Baseline (configuration management)&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt109" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;templatestyles&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;src&quot;:&quot;Module:Hatnote/styles.css&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;&quot;}}"></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt108" id="mwAWo">Main article: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baseline_(configuration_management)" title="Baseline (configuration management)">Baseline (configuration management)</a></div>

<p id="mwAWs">An approved revision of a document or source file to which subsequent changes can be made.  See <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="#Baselines,_labels_and_tags" class="mw-selflink-fragment" id="mwAWw">baselines, labels and tags</a>.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="27" id="mwAW0" aria-labelledby="Blame"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Blame">Blame</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Blame"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAW4">A search for the author and revision that last modified a particular line.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="28" id="mwAW8" aria-labelledby="Branch"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Branch">Branch</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Branch"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt110" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAXA" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Main&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Main&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Branching (revision control)&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt111" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;templatestyles&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;src&quot;:&quot;Module:Hatnote/styles.css&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;&quot;}}"></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt110" id="mwAXE">Main article: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Branching_(revision_control)" title="Branching (revision control)" class="mw-redirect">Branching (revision control)</a></div>
 
<p id="mwAXI">A set of files under version control may be <i id="mwAXM">branched</i> or <i id="mwAXQ">forked</i> at a point in time so that, from that time forward, two copies of those files may develop at different speeds or in different ways independently of each other.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="29" id="mwAXU" aria-labelledby="Change"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Change">Change</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Change"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAXY">A <i id="mwAXc">change</i> (or <i id="mwAXg"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diff" title="Diff" id="mwAXk">diff</a></i>, or <i id="mwAXo"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delta_encoding" title="Delta encoding" id="mwAXs">delta</a></i>) represents a specific modification to a document under version control.  The granularity of the modification considered a change varies between version control systems.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="30" id="mwAXw" aria-labelledby="Change_list"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Change_list">Change list</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Change list"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAX0">On many version control systems with <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atomic_transaction" title="Atomic transaction" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAX4">atomic</a> multi-change commits, a <i id="mwAX8">change list</i> (or <i id="mwAYA">CL</i>), <i id="mwAYE">change set</i>, <i id="mwAYI">update</i>, or <i id="mwAYM"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patch_(computing)" title="Patch (computing)" id="mwAYQ">patch</a></i> identifies the set of <i id="mwAYU">changes</i> made in a single commit.  This can also represent a sequential view of the source code, allowing the examination of source as of any particular changelist ID.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="31" id="mwAYY" aria-labelledby="Checkout"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Checkout">Checkout</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Checkout"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAYc">To <i id="mwAYg">check out</i> (or <i id="mwAYk">co</i>) is to create a local working copy from the repository. A user may specify a specific revision or obtain the latest. The term 'checkout' can also be used as a noun to describe the working copy. When a file has been checked out from a shared file server, it cannot be edited by other users. Think of it like a hotel, when you check out,  you no longer have access to its amenities.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="32" id="mwAYo" aria-labelledby="Clone"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Clone">Clone</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Clone"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAYs"><i id="mwAYw">Cloning</i> means creating a repository containing the revisions from another repository. This is equivalent to <i id="mwAY0">push</i>ing or <i id="mwAY4">pull</i>ing into an empty (newly initialized) repository. As a noun, two repositories can be said to be <i id="mwAY8">clone</i>s if they are kept synchronized, and contain the same revisions.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="33" id="mwAZA" aria-labelledby="Commit_(noun)"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Commit_(noun)"><span id="Commit_.28noun.29" typeof="mw:FallbackId"></span>Commit (noun)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Version_control&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Commit (noun)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<div class="excerpt-block" about="#mwt112" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAZE" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Excerpt&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Excerpt&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Changeset&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><span class="mw-empty-elt"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1353838603" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" about="#mwt113" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;templatestyles&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;src&quot;:&quot;Module:Excerpt/styles.css&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;&quot;}}">.mw-parser-output .excerpt-hat .mw-editsection-like{font-style:normal}</style><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt114" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;templatestyles&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;src&quot;:&quot;Module:Hatnote/styles.css&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;&quot;}}"></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable dablink excerpt-hat selfref">This section is an excerpt from <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Changeset" title="Changeset">Changeset</a>.<span class="mw-editsection-like plainlinks"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a rel="mw:ExtLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Changeset&amp;action=edit" class="external text" data-mw-original-href="//en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Changeset&amp;action=edit">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div><div class="excerpt">
